&lt;project_context>
# 项目: provenance_weight_training

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: westd-41312
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh connect.westd.seetacloud.com  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/provenance_weight_training


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
**任务：Figure 数据源验证 + 匿名仓库检查**

DDL 明天（EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25）。

**Part A: Figure 数据源验证**

1. 在服务器 westd-41312 上找到 figure 生成脚本：
   - 先检查 `/root/autodl-tmp/provenance_weight_training/` 下的 scripts/ 或 figures/ 目录
   - 也检查本地 `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_10/projects/provenance_weight_training/docs/paper/figures/` 是否有生成脚本
   - 搜索 `generate_paper_figures.py` 或类似文件名

2. 读取 figure 生成脚本，提取所有硬编码的数值（PPL、Δ、百分比等）

3. 将硬编码数值与 registry 实验结果交叉验证：
   - ROADMAP 关键数据：
     - Adaptive B=4: 17.674±0.007, Uniform B=4: 17.694±0.008
     - Δ(B=4)=-0.020, Δ(B=8)=-0.052, Δ(B=16)=-0.068
     - α_eff-only: 17.742, ESS-only: 17.737
     - Constraint regime: ~50% α_eff / ~44% ESS / ~6% clean
     - Sharpening ratio: ≈5.9×

4. 检查是否有 csv 数据文件被 figure 脚本引用，如有则交叉验证

5. 也检查 Figure caption（在 .tex 文件中）里的数字与图中数据是否一致

**Part B: 匿名仓库检查**

1. 检查匿名 GitHub 仓库状态：`https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026/provenance_weight_training`
2. 确认仓库是否 public 可访问
3. 确认最新 commit 是否包含所有代码文件（144 files）
4. 如果仓库不可访问或需要 push，报告具体问题

用 ssh_read_only 检查服务器上的文件。用 WebFetch 检查匿名仓库。

**输出**：将报告写入 `artifacts/audit_figures_repo.md`，用 `save_project_file` 保存。

列出：
- Figure 脚本中的硬编码数值 vs registry 值（逐个对比）
- 不一致项（如有）
- 匿名仓库状态

Degradation zone  b >= 2 (gray hatching for B&W)\n    ax.axvspan(2.0, 5.8, alpha=0.08, color='#999999', zorder=0)\n    ax.fill_between([2.0, 5.8], 17.62, 17.85, alpha=0.0, hatch='///',\n                    edgecolor='#cccccc', linewidth=0.5, zorder=0)\n\n    # ESS-only b* mean marker — black for B&W\n    yb = 17.835\n    ax.annotate(r'ESS-only $\\bar{b}^*{\\approx}3.5$',\n                xy=(3.5, 17.774), xytext=(4.3, yb),\n                fontsize=8, color='black', ha='center',\n                arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color='black', lw=0.7))\n\n    # Uniform baseline\n    ax.axhline(uniform, color=C['gray'], ls='--', lw=0.8, zorder=1,\n               label=f'Uniform ({uniform:.3f})')\n\n    # Adaptive b* vertical indicator — black dash-dot for B&W\n    ax.axvline(0.9, color='black', ls='-.', lw=0.8, alpha=0.7, zorder=1)\n    ax.text(0.80, 17.72, r'Adaptive $\\bar{b}^*{\\approx}0.9$', fontsize=8,\n            color='black', va='bottom', rotation=90)\n\n    # Main curve — seed 42 (filled black circles)\n    ax.plot(b, ppl, 'o-', color='black', markersize=4, zorder=3,\n            markerfacecolor='black', markeredgecolor='black', markeredgewidth=0.3,\n            label='Seed 42 (full grid)')\n\n    # Cross-seed at b = 0.5 — distinct markers for B&W\n    ax.plot(0.5, 17.638, 's', color='black', markersize=5, zorder=3,\n            markerfacecolor='white', markeredgecolor='black', markeredgewidth=1.0,\n            label='Seed 123')\n    ax.plot(0.5, 17.650, 'D', color=C['gray'], markersize=4.5, zorder=3,\n            markerfacecolor=C['gray'], markeredgecolor='black', markeredgewidth=0.5,\n            label='Seed 456')\n\n    ax.set_xlabel(r'Sharpening exponent $b$')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Held-out PPL')\n    ax.set_xlim(-0.2, 5.7)\n    ax.set_ylim(17.62, 17.85)\n    ax.legend(loc='upper left', framealpha=0.9, edgecolor='none',\n              handlelength=1.5, handletextpad=0.4, borderpad=0.4)\n    ax.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.FormatStrFormatter('%.2f'))\n    ax.grid(axis='y', lw=0.3, alpha=0.4, color='#ccc')\n    ax.set_axisbelow(True)\n    fig.tight_layout(pad=0.3)\n    _save(fig, 'grid_b_trend_clean')\n\n\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# Figure 2 — constraint_regime_decomposition  (appendix)\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef fig2_constraint_regime():\n    labels = [r'$\\rho{=}0.4$', r'$\\rho{=}0.6$']\n    aeff   = [38.2, 33.4]          # Section 4.4\n    ess    = [59.8, 66.0]          # derived: 100 - aeff - clean\n    clean  = [ 2.0,  0.6]          # ~2% at rho=0.4\n\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(2.8, 2.6))\n    x = np.arange(len(labels))\n    w = 0.52\n\n    ax.bar(x, aeff, w, color=C['lt_orange'], edgecolor='white', lw=0.5,\n           label=r'$\\alpha_{\\mathrm{eff}}$-binding', zorder=2)\n    ax.bar(x, ess, w, bottom=aeff, color=C['lt_blue'], edgecolor='white', lw=0.5,\n           label='ESS-binding', zorder=2)\n    ax.bar(x, clean, w, bottom=[a+e for a, e in zip(aeff, ess)],\n           color=C['green'], alpha=0.8, edgecolor='white', lw=0.5,\n           label='Clean', zorder=2)\n\n    for i in range(2):\n        ax.text(i, aeff[i] / 2, f'{aeff[i]:.1f}%',\n                ha='center', va='center', fontsize=8,\n                fontweight='bold', color='white')\n        ax.text(i, aeff[i] + ess[i] / 2, f'{ess[i]:.0f}%',\n                ha='center', va='center', fontsize=8,\n                fontweight='bold', color='white')\n\n    ax.set_ylabel('Batch proportion (%)')\n    ax.set_xticks(x)\n    ax.set_xticklabels(labels)\n    ax.set_ylim(0, 108)\n    ax.legend(loc='upper right', fontsize=8, framealpha=0.9, edgecolor='none')\n    ax.grid(axis='y', lw=0.3, alpha=0.4, color='#ccc')\n    ax.set_axisbelow(True)\n    fig.tight_layout(pad=0.3)\n    _save(fig, 'constraint_regime_decomposition')\n\n\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# Figure 3 — tau_rho_rank_preservation  (appendix, 2-panel)\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef fig3_tau_rho():\n    rho04 = {0.50: 17.688,  0.60: 17.6805, 0.70: 17.6793,\n             0.80: 17.6527, 0.90: 17.712,  0.95: 17.723}\n    rho06 = {0.60: 17.7359, 0.70: 17.730,  0.80: 17.740}\n    u04, u06 = 17.6939, 17.766\n\n    fig, (ax1, ax2) = plt.subplots(\n        1, 2, figsize=(6.8, 2.6),\n        gridspec_kw={'width_ratios': [1.3, 1], 'wspace': 0.40})\n\n    # ── Panel (a): tau-PPL curves ──\n    t04, p04 = zip(*sorted(rho04.items()))\n    t06, p06 = zip(*sorted(rho06.items()))\n\n    ax1.axvspan(0.5, 0.8, alpha=0.07, color=C['green'], zorder=0)\n    ax1.text(0.58, 17.648, 'safe range', fontsize=8,\n             color=C['teal'], fontstyle='italic')\n\n    ax1.plot(t04, p04, 'o-', color=C['blue'], markersize=4.5,\n             markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.3,\n             label=r'$\\rho{=}0.4$', zorder=3)\n    ax1.plot(t06, p06, 's-', color=C['red'], markersize=4.5,\n             markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.3,\n             label=r'$\\rho{=}0.6$', zorder=3)\n\n    # Uniform baselines\n    ax1.axhline(u04, color=C['blue'], ls='--', lw=0.7, alpha=0.5)\n    ax1.axhline(u06, color=C['red'],  ls='--', lw=0.7, alpha=0.5)\n    ax1.text(0.96, u04 + 0.001, f'{u04:.3f}', fontsize=8,\n             color=C['blue'], alpha=0.7, ha='right', va='bottom')\n    ax1.text(0.96, u06 + 0.001, f'{u06:.3f}', fontsize=8,\n             color=C['red'],  alpha=0.7, ha='right', va='bottom')\n\n    # Optimal markers\n    ax1.plot(0.8, rho04[0.8], '*', color=C['blue'], markersize=10,\n             markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.3, zorder=4)\n    ax1.plot(0.7, rho06[0.7], '*', color=C['red'], markersize=10,\n             markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.3, zorder=4)\n    ax1.annotate(r'$\\tau^*{=}0.8$', xy=(0.8, rho04[0.8]),\n                 xytext=(0.84, 17.646), fontsize=8, color=C['blue'],\n                 arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color=C['blue'], lw=0.7))\n    ax1.annotate(r'$\\tau^*{=}0.7$', xy=(0.7, rho06[0.7]),\n                 xytext=(0.75, 17.722), fontsize=8, color=C['red'],\n                 arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color=C['red'], lw=0.7))\n\n    ax1.set_xlabel(r'Quality threshold $\\tau$')\n    ax1.set_ylabel('Held-out PPL')\n    ax1.set_title('(a)', loc='left', fontweight='bold')\n    ax1.set_xlim(0.45, 0.99)\n    ax1.legend(loc='upper left', fontsize=7, framealpha=0.9, edgecolor='none')\n    ax1.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.FormatStrFormatter('%.2f'))\n    ax1.grid(axis='y', lw=0.3, alpha=0.4, color='#ccc')\n    ax1.set_axisbelow(True)\n\n    # ── Panel (b): normalized gap ──\n    shared = [0.6, 0.7, 0.8]\n    g04 = [(rho04[t] - u04) / u04 * 100 for t in shared]\n    g06 = [(rho06[t] - u06) / u06 * 100 for t in shared]\n    bw = 0.28\n    xb = np.arange(len(shared))\n\n    b1 = ax2.bar(xb - bw/2, g04, bw, color=C['blue'], alpha=0.85,\n                 label=r'$\\rho{=}0.4$')\n    b2 = ax2.bar(xb + bw/2, g06, bw, color=C['red'],  alpha=0.85,\n                 label=r'$\\rho{=}0.6$')\n\n    ax2.axhline(0, color='black', lw=0.5)\n    ax2.set_xlabel(r'Quality threshold $\\tau$')\n    ax2.set_ylabel('Gap vs. uniform (%)')\n    ax2.set_title('(b)', loc='left', fontweight='bold')\n    ax2.set_xticks(xb)\n    ax2.set_xticklabels([str(t) for t in shared])\n    ax2.set_xlim(-0.5, 2.7)\n    ax2.legend(loc='upper right', fontsize=8, framealpha=0.9, edgecolor='none')\n    ax2.grid(axis='y', lw=0.3, alpha=0.4, color='#ccc')\n    ax2.set_axisbelow(True)\n    ax2.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.FormatStrFormatter('%.2f'))\n\n    fig.subplots_adjust(left=0.08, right=0.97, bottom=0.18, top=0.93, wspace=0.40)\n    _save(fig, 'tau_rho_rank_preservation')\n\n\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# Figure 4 — dr_dual_curve  (appendix)\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef fig4_dr_dual():\n    # Appendix Table dr_threshold — all 9 thresholds\n    ret_raw = [15.0, 23, 31.5, 38, 41.4, 46, 49.8, 56, 61.6]\n    ppl_raw = [17.40, 17.27, 17.250, 17.263, 17.28, 17.30, 17.32, 17.34, 17.42]\n\n    # Epoch-matched subset\n    ret_em = [15.0, 31.5, 41.4, 49.8, 61.6]\n    ppl_em = [18.04, 17.74, 17.65, 17.62, 17.628]\n\n    adaptive, uniform = 17.651, 17.694\n\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(3.5, 2.8))\n\n    # Saturation region shading\n    ax.fill_between([47, 66], 17.615, 17.66, alpha=0.10,\n                    color=C['orange'], zorder=0)\n    ax.text(56.5, 17.637, 'saturation', ha='center', va='center',\n            fontsize=8, color=C['orange'], fontstyle='italic')\n\n    # Raw (multi-epoch) curve\n    ax.plot(ret_raw, ppl_raw, 'o-', color=C['blue'], markersize=4.5, lw=1.6,\n            markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.3,\n            label='D&R (multi-epoch)', zorder=3)\n\n    # Epoch-matched curve\n    ax.plot(ret_em, ppl_em, 's--', color=C['orange'], markersize=4.5, lw=1.6,\n            markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.3,\n            label='D&R (single epoch)', zorder=3)\n\n    # Reference lines\n    ax.axhline(adaptive, color=C['teal'], ls='-.', lw=1.0, zorder=1,\n               label=r'Adaptive $b^*$ ' + f'({adaptive})')\n    ax.axhline(uniform, color=C['gray'], ls=':', lw=1.0, zorder=1,\n               label=f'Uniform ({uniform})')\n\n    # Epoch count annotations (selected points)\n    for ret_val, label in [(15.0, '6.7x'), (31.5, '3.2x'), (61.6, '1.6x')]:\n        idx = ret_raw.index(ret_val)\n        dy = -0.04 if ret_val == 15.0 else 0.025\n        va = 'top' if ret_val == 15.0 else 'bottom'\n        ax.text(ret_val, ppl_raw[idx] + dy, label, fontsize=8,\n                color=C['blue'], ha='center', fontweight='bold', va=va)\n\n    # Best raw threshold annotation\n    ax.annotate(r'$q{>}0.3$', xy=(31.5, 17.250),\n                xytext=(21, 17.19), fontsize=8, color=C['blue'],\n                arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color=C['blue'], lw=0.7),\n                ha='center')\n\n    ax.set_xlabel('Data retained (%)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('Held-out PPL')\n    ax.set_xlim(10, 66)\n    ax.set_ylim(17.15, 18.10)\n    ax.legend(loc='upper right', fontsize=8, framealpha=0.9, edgecolor='none')\n    ax.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.FormatStrFormatter('%.2f'))\n    ax.grid(axis='y', lw=0.3, alpha=0.4, color='#ccc')\n    ax.set_axisbelow(True)\n    fig.tight_layout(pad=0.3)\n    _save(fig, 'dr_dual_curve')\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    print('Generating paper figures...')\n    fig1_grid_b_trend()\n    fig2_constraint_regime()\n    fig3_tau_rho()\n    fig4_dr_dual()\n    print('All 4 figures generated.')\n",

τ=0.8\n\n# Data ρ=0.6\ntau_06 = [0.6, 0.7, 0.8]\nppl_06 = [17.736, 17.730, 17.740]\nuniform_06 = 17.766\nbest_idx_06 = 1  # τ=0.7\n\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6, 4))\n\n# Safe range shading [0.5, 0.8] for ρ=0.4\nax.axvspan(0.5, 0.8, alpha=0.08, color='#1f77b4', label='Safe range [0.5, 0.8]')\n\n# ρ=0.4\nax.plot(tau_04, ppl_04, 'o-', color='#1f77b4', linewidth=1.8, markersize=5, label=r'$\\rho=0.4$')\nax.plot(tau_04[best_idx_04], ppl_04[best_idx_04], '*', color='#1f77b4', markersize=14, zorder=5)\nax.axhline(uniform_04, color='#1f77b4', linestyle='--', linewidth=1.0, alpha=0.6,\n           label=f'Uniform ($\\\\rho$=0.4): {uniform_04:.3f}')\n\n# ρ=0.6\nax.plot(tau_06, ppl_06, 's-', color='#d62728', linewidth=1.8, markersize=5, label=r'$\\rho=0.6$')\nax.plot(tau_06[best_idx_06], ppl_06[best_idx_06], '*', color='#d62728', markersize=14, zorder=5)\nax.axhline(uniform_06, color='#d62728', linestyle='--', linewidth=1.0, alpha=0.6,\n           label=f'Uniform ($\\\\rho$=0.6): {uniform_06:.3f}')\n\n# Annotate optimal τ\nax.annotate(r'$\\tau^*=0.8$', xy=(0.8, 17.653), xytext=(0.85, 17.645),\n            fontsize=9, color='#1f77b4',\n            arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color='#1f77b4', lw=1.0))\nax.annotate(r'$\\tau^*=0.7$', xy=(0.7, 17.730), xytext=(0.75, 17.722),\n            fontsize=9, color='#d62728',\n            arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle='->', color='#d62728', lw=1.0))\n\nax.set_xlabel(r'Threshold $\\tau$')\nax.set_ylabel('Eval PPL (holdout)')\nax.set_xlim(0.45, 1.0)\nax.legend(loc='upper right', framealpha=0.9)\n\nplt.tight_layout()\nplt.savefig('artifacts/figures/tau_ppl_curve.pdf')\nplt.savefig('artifacts/figures/tau_ppl_curve.png')\nprint(\"Saved tau_ppl_curve.pdf / .png\")\n",

= [s42[i], s123[i]]\\n        if s456_raw[i] is not None:\\n            vals.append(s456_raw[i])\\n        means.append(np.mean(vals))\\n        stds.append(np.std(vals, ddof=1) if len(vals) > 1 else 0)\\n    means = np.array(means)\\n    stds = np.array(stds)\\n\\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(5, 3.5))\\n\\n    ax.errorbar(b_vals, means, yerr=stds, fmt='o-', color='#2166AC',\\n linestyle=':', linewidth=0.8,\\n                   alpha=0.5, zorder=1)\\n    if d.get('pending_b'):\\n        ax.axvline(x=d['pending_b'][0], color='#999', linestyle=':',\\n                   linewidth=0.8, alpha=0.5, label='Pending', zorder=1)\\n\\n    ub = d['uniform_mean']\\n    ax.axhline(y=ub, color='#B2182B', linestyle='--', linewidth=1.2,\\n               label=f'Uniform ({ub:.4f})', zorder=2)\\n\\n    ab = d['adaptive_mean']\\n    ax.axhline(y=ab, color='#4DAF4A', linestyle='-.', linewidth=1.5,\\n               label=f'Adaptive $b^*$ ({ab:.4f})', zorder=2)\\n\\n    ax.set_xlabel('$b$ (downweighting strength)')\\n    ax.set_ylabel('Eval Perplexity')\\n    ax.legend(loc='upper left', fontsize=8, framealpha=0.9)\\n    ax.set_xlim(-0.2, 5.5)\\n    fig.tight_layout()\\n\\n    print('Fig 2: b curve')\\n    save_fig(fig, output_dir, 'fig2_b_curve')\\n\\n\\ndef fig_method_comparison(data, output_dir):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Figure 3: method comparison bar chart with individual points.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    d = data['method_comparison']\\n    keys = ['uniform', 'ess_only', 'adaptive_dual']\\n    labels = d['methods']\\n\\n    means = [np.mean(d[k]) for k in keys]\\n    stds = [np.std(d[k], ddof=1) for k in keys]\\n\\n    colors = ['#B2182B', '#F4A582', '#2166AC']\\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(4.5, 3.5))\\n\\n    x = np.arange(len(labels))\\n    bars = ax.bar(x, means, width=0.5, color=colors, edgecolor='#333',\\n                  linewidth=0.5, yerr=stds, capsize=4,\\n                  error_kw={'linewidth': 1, 'color': '#333'}, zorder=3)\\n\\n    for i, k in enumerate(keys):\\n        jitter = np.random.default_rng(0).uniform(-0.08, 0.08, len(d[k]))\\n        ax.scatter(x[i] + jitter, d[k], color='white', edgecolor=colors[i],\\n                   s=25, linewidth=1, zorder=4)\\n\\n    for bar, m, s in zip(bars, means, stds):\\n        ax.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width() / 2, bar.get_height() + s + 0.003,\\n                f'{m:.4f}', ha='center', va='bottom', fontsize=8, fontweight='bold')\\n\\n    ax.set_xticks(x)\\n    ax.set_xticklabels(labels, fontsize=9)\\n    ax.set_ylabel('Eval Perplexity')\\n\\n    all_vals = [v for k in keys for v in d[k]]\\n    ax.set_ylim(min(all_vals) - 0.03, max(all_vals) + 0.04)\\n\\n    delta_uni = means[0] - means[2]\\n    delta_ess = means[1] - {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Extract per-batch figure data from step_metrics.jsonl files.\\n\\nHandles restart-induced duplicates by identifying run boundaries\\n(step number decreases) and combining the initial run's early steps\\nwith the final run's later steps.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport json\\nimport csv\\nimport os\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\n\\nBASE = \\\"/root/provenance_weight_training/output/models\\\"\\nOUT = \\\"/root/provenance_weight_training/artifacts/figure_data\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef classify_regime(b, alpha_eff, tau=0.8):\\n    if b == 0.0:\\n        return \\\"clean\\\"\\n    if alpha_eff >= tau - 0.005:\\n        return \\\"alpha_eff_binding\\\"\\n    return \\\"ess_binding\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef find_run_boundaries(data):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Find indices where a new run starts (step number decreases).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    boundaries = [0]\\n    for i in range(1, len(data)):\\n        if data[i][\\\"step\\\"] < data[i - 1][\\\"step\\\"]:\\n            boundaries.append(i)\\n    return boundaries\\n\\n\\ndef load_deduped(path):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load step_metrics.jsonl with proper dedup.\\n\\n    Strategy: identify run boundaries, use the initial run for early steps\\n    and the final run for later steps. Take first 4 entries per step\\n    (one per micro-batch in gradient accumulation).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    with open(path) as f:\\n        data = [json.loads(line) for line in f]\\n\\n    if len(data) == 0:\\n        return []\\n\\n    boundaries = find_run_boundaries(data)\\n\\n    if len(boundaries) == 1:\\n        # Single run, just take first 4 per step\\n        step_entries = Excellent. Now let me compile the complete report with all figure captions and associated numbers:

## Summary of Figure-Related Content in LaTeX Files

Based on my comprehensive search through all .tex files in the project, here are all the figures with their captions and associated hardcoded numbers:

### **Figures with Captions (docs/paper/sections/)**

#### **1. Figure: grid_b_trend (results.tex, Line 81-88)**
**Caption:** "Held-out PPL as a function of fixed sharpening exponent $b$ (grid search, $\rho{=}0.4$). Seed\,42 provides the full nine-point sweep (circles); cross-seed markers at $b{=}0.5$ (triangles) confirm the plateau region. The shaded region marks $b \geq 2$ where PPL degrades past the uniform baseline; ESS-only $\bstar$ (overall mean ${\approx}$3.5) falls in this degradation zone, while adaptive $\bstar$ (mean ${\approx}$0.9) operates near the curve minimum."

**Hardcoded Numbers in Caption:**
- $b \geq 2$
- ESS-only mean ≈ 3.5
- adaptive mean ≈ 0.9

**Associated Numbers in Text (results.tex):**
- Line 10: $\sigma{=}0.033$ vs. 0.007 (cross-seed variance)
- Line 10: "noisy PPL plateau at $b \in [0.1, 0.5]$"

---

#### **2. Figure: regime_decomp (appendix.tex, Line 139-146)**
**Caption:** "Constraint regime decomposition at two contamination levels (seed\,42). At $\rho{=}0.4$, ${\sim}$50\% of batches are $\aeff$-binding and ${\sim}$44\% ESS-binding, with ${\sim}$6\% clean ($\bstar{=}0$); the quality constraint dominates. At $\rho{=}0.6$, the constraints are nearly balanced: ${\sim}$49\% ESS-binding and ${\sim}$47\% $\aeff$-binding, with ${\sim}$5\% clean; ESS-binding increases modestly from $\rho{=}0.4$ as contamination raises the stability pressure."

**Hardcoded Percentages in Caption:**
- ρ = 0.4: ~50% aeff-binding, ~44% ESS-binding, ~6% clean
- ρ = 0.6: ~49% ESS-binding, ~47% aeff-binding, ~5% clean

---

#### **3. Figure: tau_rho_rank (appendix.tex, Line 153-159)**
**Caption:** "(a) Held-out PPL as a function of quality threshold $\tau$ at $\rho \in \{0.4, 0.6\}$. $\tau{=}0.7$ and $\tau{=}0.8$ yield comparable holdout PPL at $\rho{=}0.4$; at $\rho{=}0.6$, $\tau{=}0.7$ is statistically better (paired $t{=}{-}4.70$, $p{<}0.05$, 3 seeds, $\Delta{=}{-}0.005$). $\tau{=}0.7$ also achieves lower OOD perplexity at both levels ($\rho{=}0.6$: $\Delta_{\text{OWT}}{=}{-}0.014$, $\Delta_{\text{Wiki}}{=}{-}0.013$, 2 seeds). (b) Normalized PPL gap relative to uniform at shared $\tau$ values."

**Hardcoded Numbers in Caption:**
- t = -4.70, p < 0.05
- Δ = -0.005 (at ρ = 0.6)
- ΔᵒWT = -0.014
- ΔWiki = -0.013

---

#### **4. Figure: dr_dual_curve (appendix.tex, Line 215-223)**
**Caption:** "D&R perplexity as a function of data retained. **Blue**: raw (multi-epoch) PPL traces a U-shaped curve; annotations show effective epoch count. **Orange**: epoch-matched (single content epoch) PPL decreases monotonically and saturates at $q{\geq}0.5$, matching the adaptive $\bstar$ level (green). The raw U-shape is entirely a multi-epoch artifact: controlling for data exposure eliminates it."

**No hardcoded numbers in caption text itself**, but referenced in table context.

---

#### **5. Figure: delta_vs_rho (appendix.tex, Line 477-486)**

130	We tested a per-batch adaptive variant that clamps $\tau$ between 0.5 and the fixed threshold based on local $\aeff(0)$, allowing the quality floor to relax in batches with low estimated real fraction.
131	At both contamination levels ($\rho \in \{0.4, 0.6\}$), the adaptive variant did not outperform the fixed threshold (single-seed exploratory at each level), suggesting the quality constraint does not require adaptation to local contamination density.
132	This supports the ``two hyperparameters with good defaults'' characterization: neither $\tau$ nor $\nmin$ benefits from per-batch tuning.
133	
134	\section{Constraint Regime Decomposition}
135	\label{app:regime}
136	
137	Figure~\ref{fig:regime_decomp} visualizes the constraint regime decomposition at two contamination levels.
138	
139	\begin{figure}[h]
140	\centering
141	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{constraint_regime_decomposition}
142	\caption{Constraint regime decomposition at two contamination levels (seed\,42).
143	At $\rho{=}0.4$, ${\sim}$50\% of batches are $\aeff$-binding and ${\sim}$44\% ESS-binding, with ${\sim}$6\% clean ($\bstar{=}0$); the quality constraint dominates.
144	At $\rho{=}0.6$, the constraints are nearly balanced: ${\sim}$49\% ESS-binding and ${\sim}$47\% $\aeff$-binding, with ${\sim}$5\% clean; ESS-binding increases modestly from $\rho{=}0.4$ as contamination raises the stability pressure.}

146	\end{figure}

152	
153	\begin{figure}[h]
154	\centering
155	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{tau_rho_rank_preservation}
156	\caption{(a) Held-out PPL as a function of quality threshold $\tau$ at $\rho \in \{0.4, 0.6\}$.
157	$\tau{=}0.7$ and $\tau{=}0.8$ yield comparable holdout PPL at $\rho{=}0.4$; at $\rho{=}0.6$, $\tau{=}0.7$ is statistically better (paired $t{=}{-}4.70$, $p{<}0.05$, 3 seeds, $\Delta{=}{-}0.005$). $\tau{=}0.7$ also achieves lower OOD perplexity at both levels ($\rho{=}0.6$: $\Delta_{\text{OWT}}{=}{-}0.014$, $\Delta_{\text{Wiki}}{=}{-}0.013$, 2 seeds).
158	(b) Normalized PPL gap relative to uniform at shared $\tau$ values.}

- ~50% aeff-binding, ~44% ESS-binding, ~6% clean

- aeff=38.2%, ess=59.8%, clean=2.0%


The figure script matches the extracted data perfectly, but there's a major mismatch with what's written in the paper. The caption claims ~50% aeff / ~44% ESS / ~6% clean, while the actual figure will display 38.2% / 59.8% / 2.0%. Looking at results.tex, the text also states 5.8% clean with ~50% aeff-binding and ~44% ESS-binding, which contradicts both the extracted data and what the figure will actually show.

The issue is that the extracted data uses tau=0.8 in the classify_regime function, but the paper's reported percentages appear to be from tau=0.7 calculations. The appendix table shows b*=0 percentages around 5.8-5.9%, which aligns with the paper's ~6% claim, not the 2.0% that the figure script is producing.

There's a fundamental mismatch between what the extraction script calculates and what the paper reports. The extraction might be operating at a different granularity level (micro-batch vs. seed-level aggregation), or handling restart deduplication in a way that changes the results. Let me check the results.tex file to see what the actual reported constraint regime numbers are.

Looking at the figure data more carefully, the script defines clean as exactly b==0.0 and shows 38.2% alpha_eff, 59.8% ESS, and 2.0% clean for ρ=0.4. But the caption and results.tex claim ~50% aeff-binding, ~44% ESS-binding, and ~6% clean—these numbers are too far apart to be rounding differences, so something fundamental is wrong with how the data is being reported versus calculated.

The discrepancy could stem from different definitions of "binding" between the figure extraction and the text analysis, or from floating-point precision issues in step_metrics.jsonl where values like 0.00001 get counted differently than exact zeros. The per-seed table's "b*=0 (%)" column might also be measuring something distinct from what the extraction script identifies as clean. Regardless, the figure will render with the extracted data, so I need to figure out which numbers are actually correct.

Wait, I should verify whether the text's ~50/44/6 percentages refer to the paper's recommended B=4, tau=0.7 setting rather than the adaptive_fix_seed42 configuration that uses tau=0.8. Since tau affects the alpha_eff threshold for binding classification, a lower tau value would shift more batches into the alpha_eff-binding category, which would explain the different regime distributions. The paper likely recommends tau=0.7, but the figure appears to use tau=0.8 data instead, which would explain the discrepancy between the ~50% alpha_eff_binding mentioned in the text versus the 38.3% shown in the figure. Now I need to compile the full audit report and double-check the file counts in the anonymous repository.

120	The default $\nmin{=}2$ ($\nmin/B{=}0.5$) is optimal; extremes degrade ($\nmin{=}1$: PPL $17.742{\pm}0.009$, 3 seeds, worse than uniform; $\nmin{=}B$: recovers uniform).
121	Neither constraint alone improves over uniform ($\nmin{=}1$: $+$0.048; ESS-only: $+$0.043), confirming the dual constraint is necessary.
122	The coupling $\nmin{=}\lfloor B/2 \rfloor$ is also empirically motivated: at $B{=}8$, keeping $\nmin{=}2$ (ratio $0.25$) degrades PPL by $+$0.036 vs.\ uniform, whereas $\nmin{=}4$ (ratio $0.5$) yields $\Delta{=}{-}0.052{\pm}0.007$.
123	Under this coupling, the adaptive advantage monotonically increases across $B \in \{4, 8, 16\}$: $\Delta{=}{-}0.020{\pm}0.008$ (3 seeds), $-$0.052${\pm}$0.007 (3 seeds, $p{=}0.006$), and $-$0.068${\pm}$0.005 (3 seeds), corresponding to $1.0{\times}$, $2.6{\times}$, and $3.4{\times}$ amplification ($p{<}0.01$ at $B \geq 8$).
124	The gap to grid-best narrows from 0.040 ($B{=}4$) to 0.009 ($B{=}16$, seed\,42).
125	
126	\paragraph{Constraint regime.}
127	\label{sec:regime}
128	Over 24{,}412 micro-batches (seed\,42), 5.8\% are clean ($\aeff(0) \geq \tau$, $\bstar{=}0$), ${\sim}$50\% are $\aeff$-binding (quality constraint dominates), and ${\sim}$44\% are ESS-binding (stability constraint dominates); the distribution is stable across seeds (CV ${<}2\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:regime}).
129	The $\aeff$-binding majority indicates that detector quality signal---not training stability---is the primary bottleneck, reinforcing the calibration requirement (\S\ref{sec:robustness}).
130

_source line 92 · 2026-05-24T04:38:44.398Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I see the full picture. Results.tex line 127-128 says:

"Over 24,412 micro-batches (seed 42), 5.8% are clean (α_eff(0) ≥ τ, b*=0), ~50% are α_eff-binding (quality constraint dominates), and ~44% are ESS-binding (stability constraint dominates)"

But the binding_stats_seed42.json from the extraction says:
- total_batches: 24412 ✓ (matches)


- alpha_eff_binding: 38.3%
- ess_binding: 59.78%
- clean: 1.91%

So the total batch count matches perfectly, but the regime percentages are completely different. The text reports 5.8% clean, ~50% alpha_eff-binding, and ~44% ESS-binding, while the extracted data shows 1.91% clean, 38.3% alpha_eff-binding, and 59.78% ESS-binding. The text defines clean as α_eff(0) ≥ τ with b*=0, but the extraction uses a different classification logic based on which constraint is binding.

The key difference is the threshold value: the extraction uses tau=0.8, which requires alpha_eff ≥ 0.795 to qualify as alpha_eff-binding, resulting in fewer batches in that regime. If the paper text used tau=0.7 instead, the threshold would be 0.695, allowing more batches to qualify as alpha_eff-binding and matching the ~50% reported. This explains why the extraction and figure script show tau=0.8 results while the paper text appears to report tau=0.7 regime statistics.

Now I'm checking whether the micro-batch count can distinguish between the two experiments. Both tau=0.8 and tau=0.7 use the same underlying data and training loop, so they'd have the same 24,412 micro-batches. But the clean percentage is the real giveaway—the text reports 5.8% clean, which matches the tau=0.7 experiment's statistics in the appendix table.

The extraction shows 1.91% clean, which aligns with tau=0.8 instead. This makes sense because at tau=0.7, the relaxed quality floor means more batches don't need reweighting (b*=0), whereas tau=0.8's stricter constraint results in fewer such batches. So the figure is pulling constraint regime data from the tau=0.8 experiment while the paper's main results and captions describe tau=0.7—that's the core problem I need to address.

Now I'm compiling the full report and checking the repository file count using a different approach since I can't use pipes in the SSH read-only environment.
```
